Multiple-choice accuracy did not materially change in the 4B study. | Requested by `default`/`thorough` (200 steps), off in `fast`. Cost is phase/model-specific: the 27B run took 247 min layer-wise plus 470.8 min final-KL, not a general +50 % overhead. Layer-sharded placement can use saved teacher head inputs (`<workdir>/qad/head_inputs.pt`); if the model-fit check still fails, final-KL is skipped with a note. See [operations.md](operations.md). |
| Norm weights in the layer-wise phase | Within seed noise | Off |
| KL term at the last layers of the layer-wise phase | Within seed noise | Off |
| 2x QAD samples (512 instead of 256) | No gain: the docs KLD is 0.0598 against 0.0607, the perplexity is higher. | Off |
| Task-style training text in the QAD mix | Worse on held-out text (docs KLD 0.0720 against 0.0607). It adapts the model to the task style, not to the language. | Not a default |

## Setup

* Model: Qwen3.5-0.8B at 3.5 bpw (error ratio chosen for that size), one V620.
* Two held-out texts: `docs` (documentation text, unquantized perplexity 10.874) and `mix`
  (mixed domains, 5.483). The columns are perplexity of the quantized model and
  KL(original to quantized) in nats per token.
* Every run uses the same calibration text, the same seed, and the `fast` QAD preset
  unless the row says otherwise.
* Seed noise: the same recipe with a different seed moves the docs KLD by about 0.001
  (0.0607 and 0.0615). Treat smaller differences as noise.

## Results on Qwen3.5-0.8B

| Run | docs ppl | docs KLD | mix ppl | mix KLD |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| PTQ only (3.5 bpw) | 12.406 | 0.1288 | 6.848 | 0.1841 |
| QAD (3.5 bpw), reference | 11.388 | 0.0607 | 6.309 | 0.1249 |
| QAD with INT8 candidates | 11.409 | 0.0604 | 6.309 | 0.1255 |
| PTQ with INT8 candidates | 12.412 | 0.1290 | 6.850 | 0.1845 |
| PTQ, 2 most sensitive tensors forced to INT8 | 12.484 | 0.1327 | 6.872 | 0.1885 |
| PTQ, 4 forced | 12.576 | 0.1443 | 7.014 | 0.2085 |
| PTQ, 8 forced | 12.774 | 0.1620 | 7.186 | 0.2379 |
| QAD, norm weights trained | 11.406 | 0.0598 | 6.298 | 0.1236 |
| QAD, KL term at the last layers | 11.376 | 0.0617 | 6.308 | 0.1250 |
| QAD, 512 samples | 11.476 | 0.0598 | 6.323 | 0.1253 |
| QAD, task-style text in the mix | 11.603 | 0.0720 | not run | not run |
| QAD + final KL, 100 steps | 11.148 | 0.0573 | 6.174 | 0.1165 |
| QAD + final KL, 200 steps | 11.133 | 0.0556 | 6.140 | 0.1142 |
| QAD + final KL, 200 steps, learning-rate variant 1 | 11.090 | 0.0522 | 6.137 | 0.1128 |
| QAD + final KL, 200 steps, learning-rate variant 2 | 11.040 | 0.0527 | 6.020 | 0.1140 |
| QAD (3.0 bpw), reference | 11.796 | 0.0993 | 7.292 | 0.2538 |

## Results on Qwen3.5-4B

Same setup at 3.5 bpw (error ratio 6.2; allocation 3.499 bpw, INT2 44.4 % and INT4 55.6 % of
the rows), unquantized perplexity 7.067 on the docs text.

| Run | docs ppl | docs KLD | mix ppl | mix KLD |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| PTQ only | 7.600 | 0.0927 | 4.055 | 0.1168 |
| PTQ with INT8 candidates | 7.604 | 0.0925 | 4.056 | 0.1173 |
| QAD, weight-unit latents (older default) | 7.190 | 0.0416 | 3.999 | 0.1033 |
| QAD, step-unit latents (current default) | 7.221 | 0.0450 | 3.985 | 0.0934 |
| QAD, step-unit latents + final KL, 200 steps | 7.137 | 0.0425 | 3.933 | 0.0869 |
| PTQ only, 3.0 bpw | 8.041 | 0.1607 | 4.438 | 0.2258 |
| QAD, step-unit latents, 3.0 bpw | 7.308 | 0.0677 | 4.276 | 0.1770 |

The final-KL phase lowers the KLD against the step-unit QAD baseline by 5.6 % (docs) and
7.0 % (mix). Its own log shows the held-out KL falling from 0.1175 to 0.1033 (-12.1 %) in
92.9 minutes. Against the older weight-unit QAD, the docs KLD does not improve (0.0425
against 0.0416). INT8 candidates change nothing at this size, as on the 0.8B model.

All numbers of this page are in
[`experiments/results/lowbit_runs_2026-10-26.json`](../experiments/results/lowbit_runs_2026-10-26.json)
(parsed from the `hi2 eval` logs of each run).

Read the 0.8B table as follows.

1. **INT8 does not help at this size.** The allocator already finds that INT4 is enough
   for almost every row. Forced INT8 costs bits that INT2 rows would have saved, so
   quality drops as more tensors are forced.
2. **The final logit-KL phase is the only option that moves the numbers.** It trains
   against the full output distribution after the layer-wise phase. It reduces KLD, but
   multiple-choice accuracy did not change in the 4B runs.
3. **Training text matters less than expected.** More samples do not help. Task-style
   text hurts held-out language modelling.

## Defaults

The final-KL phase is on with the default QAD preset. The other options are off. Switch with:

```bash
python -m hi2 quantize SRC DST --calib calib.txt --bits 2,4,8           # INT8 rows
python -m hi2 quantize SRC DST --calib calib.txt --qad-final-kl 0       # turn the final logit-KL phase off
```

The final-KL learning rates default to the values measured on Qwen3.5-4B.

## Tools

* `experiments/restamp_qad.py`: reuse per-layer QAD artifacts of an older run after
  checking that the recomputed layers are bit-identical.
* `experiments/int8_usage.py`, `experiments/make_calib_tasks.py`: INT8 usage report and
  task-style calibration text.
* Tests: `tests/test_lowbit.py`.

## What is not done

* The 4B final-KL runs at 100 steps and the 4B learning-rate run were stopped before they
  finished. The 4B numbers come from the 200-step run only.
* Nobody scored the 0.8B final-KL variants on the multiple-choice tasks.


<!-- docs/mcp.md -->
# Local MCP adapter

`hi2-mcp` is an optional, local Model Context Protocol (MCP) adapter. It uses
standard input/output (**stdio**) and the same service and durable job registry
as hi2. It does not accept shell commands, Python functions, remote model IDs,
credentials, or arbitrary resource filenames. The baseline workflow returns
job IDs in ordinary MCP tool results. The optional **Tasks** extension is not
required.

Inspection and planning read checkpoint metadata but do not quantize. Quantization,
held-out quality evaluation, structural validation, and portable export return a
job handle; detached local supervisors own the work after the client disconnects.
A successful structural check or dense numerical evaluation is **not** evidence
of native vLLM/kernel execution, native memory use, throughput, or platform support.

## Installation and compatibility

Follow the [installation guide](installation.md#checkout-and-virtual-environment),
including its optional MCP extra, in the Python environment that will launch the
server. Use the combined test/MCP extras when running adapter tests.

The extra pins the **official `mcp==2.3.0` Python SDK**, using
`mcp.server.MCPServer` rather than the legacy `FastMCP` API. The same package supplies
the Python `Client` below.
Importing `hi2.mcp_server` does not import the SDK or Torch; invoking the adapter
without the extra exits with an installation command on stderr. Other SDK
versions are rejected rather than silently using unverified transport internals.

| Component | Intended compatibility | Actual smoke-test status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Python SDK/server | `mcp==2.3.0` | Editable extra installation and `hi2-mcp --help` passed on Python 3.12.13, Darwin arm64 |
| Python client | `mcp==2.3.0`, `mcp.Client` with `StdioServerParameters` | Standalone discover → plan → submit → disconnect → reconnect → validate → evaluate → portable workflow passed |
| MCP protocol | `2026-07-28`, negotiated by the SDK client | Observed in the standalone stdio session; negotiated Tasks tests passed in the CPU suite |
| CPU fixture | Locally generated, one-layer Llama safetensors and local tokenizer; no downloads | Queued job survived reconnect with the same ID; quality gate passed; cancellation acknowledged `cancelling`, then reached `cancelled` |
| Native accelerator/runtime | No claim | Not measured |

Verified on **2026-11-04**: the complete CPU/MCP suite passed **941 tests**, with
61 GPU/HIP, multi-GPU, GPU-pinned-memory or unavailable-Triton skips. The MCP extra
was installed, so protocol tests executed rather than skipping. The documented
discover → plan → submit → disconnect → reconnect → validate client also passed
again on November 4. The expanded standalone evaluate/export/cancellation
observations in the table were recorded on November 3; those behaviors also have
coverage in the current suite. Native execution remains unmeasured. Rerun the
focused protocol suite with:

```sh
python -m pytest -q tests/test_mcp_server.py tests/test_mcp_tasks.py
```

The tests require the extra and CPU PyTorch/Transformers dependencies. With no
MCP extra installed, optional adapter tests are skipped; that skip is not MCP
acceptance evidence. No GPU, downloaded model, network service, or credentials
are needed. The adapter targets local POSIX hosts (Linux/macOS): fd isolation,
`fcntl.flock`, SQLite, inherited ownership descriptors, detached child sessions
and Unix signals are required. Keep the launching interpreter/checkout available
until jobs finish. The process tests also exercise FIFOs and stop/continue/kill
signals, so they need a host that permits real child-process lifecycle operations.
This is not a Windows or remotely exposed service. See
[focused regression suites](usage.md#development-tests) and
[durable-job prerequisites](usage.md#durable-local-jobs).

## Operator-owned roots

The operator grants access when launching the process, not through tool arguments:

```sh
hi2-mcp \
  --model-root /srv/hi2/models \
  --calibration-root /srv/hi2/calibration \
  --output-root /srv/hi2/outputs \
  --state-root /srv/hi2/private-state
```

Equivalently use `python -m hi2.mcp_server` with those arguments. Paths must be
absolute, without parent traversal. Each `--model-root`, `--calibration-root`, and
`--output-root` flag may be repeated. An omitted root category grants no access to
that category. `--state-root` is required and must be outside the input/output resource trees.
The process creates a missing state directory privately; an existing one must be
operator-owned, mode **0700**, and not a symlink. Do not use a shared writable
state directory. Jobs live only in `<state-root>/jobs`.

`WorkspacePolicy` checks paths and registered IDs against the configured roots,
including symlink escapes. Model and calibration inputs must already exist.
Outputs must be subdirectories of output roots and must not overlap inputs or
private state. Tools can read safetensors metadata; they do not accept untrusted
pickle/cache payloads as resources.

The **output** holds the checkpoint and published reports. The **workdir** holds
run recovery state. The **cache** holds reusable phase products. Recipes cannot
set workdir or cache paths: the application assigns deterministic private
directories under the state root. Planning may create these private directories
and persist resource registrations. It does not create a checkpoint or submit
a job.

Quantizer-created ancillary links may point into configured model roots: tokenizer
and template assets remain read-only model capabilities. Checkpoint weights,
configuration and run evidence cannot use that exception. Portable export
materializes its recognized assets. Model loading explicitly disables remote code;
manifest calibration paths are checked against calibration roots before evaluation.

This is a local capability boundary, not an OS sandbox against other hostile
processes running as the same user. Protect the input roots and private state
from concurrent modification by untrusted users. Client MCP roots do not expand
these operator grants. Changing roots on reconnect may deliberately invalidate
previous registrations. Never delete active job state to "reset" a client;
process ownership and leases depend on it.

## Tool contracts

Discover current input/output JSON Schemas with `list_tools()`. The SDK validates
the typed signatures and results; unknown arguments, unknown recipe fields,
unknown nested encoder/distillation fields, invalid enums, nonfinite numbers,
and out-of-range pagination/evaluation settings are rejected. Recipe fields are
derived from the shared service dataclasses rather than a second configuration
language. WorkspacePolicy and service preflight additionally enforce path
capabilities and semantic constraints before submitting work.

Recipes are service configuration, not a serialization of CLI flags. `devices` is
a list of `cpu`/`cuda`/`cuda:N` strings, never `"auto"` or a comma-separated string;
`calib` is one rooted path/ID or a list (up to 32). Encoding settings are nested in
`cfg`; `qad` is a distillation-settings object or `null`. Omitting `qad` leaves QAD
off, unlike calibrated CLI `quantize`, which resolves a preset by default. For a
CLI-planned recipe, omit `workdir`, `cache_dir`, and `calib_ids` before submission:
they are excluded from the MCP input schema even if null. Keep the remaining
resolved settings explicit. `--target-kld` is a CLI tuning workflow, not a recipe
field. `hi2_plan.summary` is compact planning output, not a replayable recipe.

All successful tool responses have `schema_version: 1`. Check `result.is_error`
before using `result.structured_content`; a protocol-level rejection instead
raises `MCPError` in the SDK client.

| Tool | Arguments | Structured result / effect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `hi2_inspect` | `path`, optional `kind: "model"` or `"artifact"` | Safetensors metadata preview, persisted `resource_id`, available evidence URIs. No tensor validation or model execution. |
| `hi2_plan` | `recipe: {schema_version: 1, config: {...}}` | Compact resolved phases/settings/estimates and `output_ids`. No job submitted. |
| `hi2_quantize` | `recipe`, `idempotency_key` | `JobHandle`, operation `quantize`. |
| `hi2_evaluate` | `request`, `idempotency_key` | `JobHandle`, operation `quality`; held-out dense numerical evaluation. |
| `hi2_status` | `job_id` | Compact durable `JobHandle`, including result/error previews and evidence URIs. |
| `hi2_cancel` | `job_id` | Current `JobHandle` after requesting cancellation; not a promise of immediate termination. |
| `hi2_validate` | artifact `path` or registered artifact ID, `idempotency_key` | `JobHandle`, operation `validate`; structural checkpoint validation runs in the worker. |
| `hi2_portable` | artifact `src`, new `out`, `idempotency_key`, optional model `assets_from` | `JobHandle`, operation `portable`; export/checking occurs in the worker. |

`hi2_evaluate.request` requires `src` (model root/ID), `dst` (artifact root/ID),
and `text` (calibration root/ID). Optional fields are `excluded` (at most 32 rooted
text paths), `max_kld` (nonnegative), `max_ppl_ratio` (positive), `samples`
(1–4096, default 8), `seq_len` (2–131072, default 2048), `device` (default `cpu`),
and `dtype` (`float32`, `float16`, or `bfloat16`, default `float32`). `device` accepts
one `cpu`, `cuda`, or `cuda:N`, not the CLI's comma-separated device list. CPU is
explicit in the example; the adapter does not alter architecture-specific dtype
support. Use held-out text separate from calibration/tuning text. The service checks
exact document overlap against recorded calibration and explicit `excluded` paths,
not semantic deduplication or unknown training data. Recorded paths must still
exist and pass the calibration-root policy.

An idempotency key contains 1–512 characters and is unique across operations in
one registry. Persist it with the recipe and job ID. Repeating the same normalized
operation/request and key returns the same job, even if it is terminal; reusing it
for different settings fails rather than starting a second computation. Keep the
same roots, state and device-visibility environment on reconnect. Workspace path
checks still apply before submission retries: a key does not bypass root policy or
restore removed inputs. Use `hi2_status` with a saved job ID to retrieve existing
job evidence. Do not put secrets into keys. Different keys still obey shared
resource leases; a retry of interrupted work needs a new key and an
identity-compatible recipe, not changed settings in an old output/workspace.

A job handle contains `job_id`, `state`, `operation`, timestamps, request digest,
`cancellation`, bounded `result`/`error` previews, and `resources` (a list of
`{name, uri}`). States are `queued`, `running`, `cancelling`, `succeeded`, `failed`,
`cancelled`, and `interrupted`. Previews may be truncated or omitted; they are not
full reports. An `interrupted` job is not a successful artifact and must not be
silently treated as resumable success.

Do not interchange these identifiers:

| Field | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `job_id` | Durable submission in this registry. Use it for status, cancellation and reconnect. It also identifies validation, evaluation and export jobs. |
| `run_id` | Deterministic identity of a quantization run's resolved recipe, inputs and implementation environment. A compatible resume keeps it. |
| `attempt_id` | One execution of that quantization run. A new execution gets a fresh value, including on resume. |

The quantization manifests `run.json` and `hi2_run.json` record the run and
attempt IDs. They are not substitutes for the job ID returned by submission.

Quality reports use `context: "dense_numerical"` and one of `target_met`,
`target_not_met`, `not_measured`, or `measured`. `measured` means measurements exist
without acceptance criteria. Rejected quality criteria can leave a readable
`hi2_quality.json` even though the job failed. Read that evidence before making a
quality decision. `native_runtime: "not_measured"` remains explicit.

## Python client: discover, inspect, plan, start, reconnect, validate

Install the same optional extra in the client environment. Save this as a local
client script, after replacing the example operator paths with your own. The
model must already be a local safetensors checkpoint; this script does not fetch
anything. The example uses data-free CPU quantization and therefore does not
need calibration text to start.

```python
import json
from pathlib import Path
import sys

import anyio
from mcp import Client, StdioServerParameters

ROOT = Path("/srv/hi2")
MODEL = ROOT / "models" / "local-model"
OUTPUT = ROOT / "outputs" / "cpu-run"
RECEIPT = Path("hi2-job-receipt.json")  # Client-owned; not a server resource.

server = StdioServerParameters(
    command=sys.executable,  # This interpreter must have hi2[mcp] installed.
    args=[
        "-m",
        "hi2.mcp_server",
        "--model-root",
        str(ROOT / "models"),
        "--calibration-root",
        str(ROOT / "calibration"),
        "--output-root",
        str(ROOT / "outputs"),
        "--state-root",
        str(ROOT / "private-state"),
    ],
    env={"HF_HUB_OFFLINE": "1", "TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE": "1"},
)
recipe = {
    "schema_version": 1,
    "config": {
        "src": str(MODEL),
        "dst": str(OUTPUT),
        "devices": ["cpu"],
        "cfg": {"effort": "fast", "rounding": "nearest", "rotation": "off"},
        "moe_layout": "per-expert",
    },
}
TERMINAL = {"succeeded", "failed", "cancelled", "interrupted"}


async def invoke(client, name, arguments):
    result = await client.call_tool(name, arguments)
    if result.is_error:
        raise RuntimeError(result.content)
    return result.structured_content


async def wait_for_job(client, job_id):
    while True:
        status = await invoke(client, "hi2_status", {"job_id": job_id})
        print(status["state"], status["resources"])
        if status["state"] in TERMINAL:
            return status
        await anyio.sleep(1)


async def main():
    async with Client(server) as client:
        print("Negotiated protocol:", client.protocol_version)
        tools = await client.list_tools()
        print([(tool.name, tool.input_schema) for tool in tools.tools])
        inspected = await invoke(client, "hi2_inspect", {"path": str(MODEL)})
        print(inspected)
        print(await invoke(client, "hi2_plan", {"recipe": recipe}))
        key = "cpu-run-001"
        handle = await invoke(
            client,
            "hi2_quantize",
            {
                "recipe": recipe,
                "idempotency_key": key,
            },
        )
        RECEIPT.write_text(
            json.dumps({"job_id": handle["job_id"], "key": key, "recipe": recipe})
        )
    # Exiting Client closes only this stdio server. Durable work is independent.

    receipt = json.loads(RECEIPT.read_text())
    async with Client(server) as client:  # Fresh process, same roots/state.
        result = await wait_for_job(client, receipt["job_id"])
        if result["state"] != "succeeded":
            raise RuntimeError(result)
        artifact = await invoke(
            client,
            "hi2_inspect",
            {
                "kind": "artifact",
                "path": str(OUTPUT),
            },
        )
        print("Persist this artifact ID and its URIs:", artifact)
        validation = await invoke(
            client,
            "hi2_validate",
            {
                "path": artifact["resource_id"],
                "idempotency_key": "cpu-run-001-validation",
            },
        )
        checked = await wait_for_job(client, validation["job_id"])
        if checked["state"] != "succeeded":
            raise RuntimeError(checked)
        for reference in artifact["resources"]:
            print(
                reference["name"],
                (await client.read_resource(reference["uri"])).contents,
            )


if __name__ == "__main__":
    anyio.run(main)
```

If a client loses the submit response, reopen `Client(server)` and repeat
`hi2_quantize` with the **same saved recipe and key**, then save the returned ID.
Do not make up a fresh key merely because the connection closed. The reference
script stores the receipt after receiving the handle; applications should persist
the intended key/recipe before submission when recovery from that specific
client-crash window is required.

A plain desktop MCP host can launch exactly the same executable and arguments.
For example, adapt this host configuration to absolute local paths (configuration
key names may differ between hosts):

```json
{
  "mcpServers": {
    "hi2": {
      "command": "/srv/hi2/venv/bin/hi2-mcp",
      "args": [
        "--model-root", "/srv/hi2/models",
        "--calibration-root", "/srv/hi2/calibration",
        "--output-root", "/srv/hi2/outputs",
        "--state-root", "/srv/hi2/private-state"
      ]
    }
  }
}
```

This is a launch example, not a claim of testing a particular desktop host/version.
The Python SDK client is the explicit compatibility target. No credentials or
shell wrappers are necessary.

## Cancellation and optional evaluation/export

Cancellation is a separate durable action, not transport-request cancellation.
Closing the client, cancelling `call_tool`, or killing its stdio server is **not**
`hi2_cancel`. From a fresh client connected with the same state root:

```python
async with Client(server) as client:
    receipt = json.loads(RECEIPT.read_text())
    response = await invoke(client, "hi2_cancel", {"job_id": receipt["job_id"]})
    print(response["state"], response["cancellation"])
    terminal = await wait_for_job(client, receipt["job_id"])
    print(terminal)
```

`cancelling` with `cancellation: "requested"` means cancellation was recorded.
It does **not** mean the worker has stopped or released its resource leases.
Poll to a terminal state. Cancellation can race success; the response can then
report `succeeded` with `cancellation: "already_finished"`. Do not accept partial
artifacts from cancelled, failed or interrupted work.

For quantization, the worker binds its supervisor token and workdir to both
`run_id` and `attempt_id`. A validated checkpoint committed by that bound
attempt remains `succeeded` if cancellation arrives before the worker receipt
or during teardown. The registry recognizes this success only after worker
ownership ends; it does not release leases early. A prior or later successful
attempt with the same `run_id` cannot complete this job.

Orphan recovery also retains a matching failed/interrupted receipt and its
original error, including quality measurements. Explicit cancellation takes
precedence over an uncommitted failure but retains the worker error.
The actual process return code is retained. It can be nonzero after a successful
commit; orphan recovery reports an unknown return code as `null`.

Ownership uses inherited file locks, not a PID or heartbeat timeout. A stopped
orphan retains leases until its ownership descriptor closes; do not delete locks
or active state to force progress. Status can restart an unstarted queue entry but
does not automatically replay an interrupted operation. Separate state registries
do not arbitrate shared devices with one another.
Detached supervisors are reaped by a background waiter when their submitter
remains alive; submission does not wait for completion, and exiting the submitter
does not stop its jobs.

After quantization succeeds, submit independent final quality evaluation with
a new key. This is dense numerical evaluation, not the final-KL training phase.
Keep its text separate from all calibration and tuning inputs:

```python
async with Client(server) as client:
    evaluation = await invoke(
        client,
        "hi2_evaluate",
        {
            "request": {
                "src": str(MODEL),
                "dst": str(OUTPUT),
                "text": str(ROOT / "calibration" / "held-out.txt"),
                "samples": 8,
                "seq_len": 128,
                "device": "cpu",
                "dtype": "float32",
                "max_kld": 0.1,
                "max_ppl_ratio": 1.1,
            },
            "idempotency_key": "cpu-run-001-held-out",
        },
    )
    print(await wait_for_job(client, evaluation["job_id"]))
    artifact = await invoke(
        client, "hi2_inspect", {"kind": "artifact", "path": str(OUTPUT)}
    )
    print(artifact["resources"])  # Includes quality when the report exists.
```

Those criteria are illustrative application choices, not promised model quality.
For export, call `hi2_portable` with `src=artifact["resource_id"]`, an unused
`out` under an output root, a fresh idempotency key, and optionally
`assets_from=str(MODEL)`. Poll its job, then inspect/register the new output.

Portable export retains the artifact's own run/quality evidence and byte-identical
HI2 weights, with a checksum manifest for copied files. Assets supplied through
`assets_from` do not replace that evidence. It does not run quality evaluation,
bundle reference-model weights/calibration corpora, rewrite original provenance
paths, or qualify a native runtime. Later evaluation needs the reference model and
recorded calibration files, still available within the configured roots; missing
files fail rather than disabling contamination checks. A report without recorded
source identity cannot establish that the reference matches the quantization source.

Re-evaluating a portable artifact updates its quality report and checksums under
the output lock. These are separate atomic replacements, not jointly hard-kill
atomic: a kill between them can leave a bundle rejected by structural/checksum
validation. Submit `hi2_validate` after an abrupt termination before trusting or
distributing it; do not remove evidence to bypass rejection. See
[quality/portable provenance](usage.md#quality-and-portable-provenance) and
[distribution guidance](operations.md#distribute-a-checkpoint).

## Read-only resources and limits

Resource templates are discoverable with `list_resource_templates()`. Tools
return concrete URIs. Resource reads cannot register new IDs or take a filesystem
path; only `hi2_inspect` registers an existing model/artifact after policy checks.
The registry persists IDs across server restarts with the same state root.

| URI | Meaning | Paging |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `hi2://jobs/{job_id}/status` | Compact durable status, identical handle shape | One bounded JSON document |
| `hi2://jobs/{job_id}/events{?cursor,limit}` | Retained progress events | Exclusive monotonic event cursor; default/max 16 events |
| `hi2://jobs/{job_id}/log{?cursor,limit}` | Private worker log only | Byte cursor; default/max 16384 bytes |
| `hi2://artifacts/{artifact_id}/manifest{?cursor,limit}` | `hi2_run.json`, or portable manifest when no run manifest exists | Byte cursor; default/max 16384 bytes |
| `hi2://artifacts/{artifact_id}/report{?cursor,limit}` | `hi2_report.json` | Byte cursor; default/max 16384 bytes |
| `hi2://artifacts/{artifact_id}/quality{?cursor,limit}` | `hi2_quality.json`, when present | Byte cursor; default/max 16384 bytes |
| `hi2://artifacts/{artifact_id}/support` | Evidence inventory, limits, quality context, explicit unmeasured-native status | One compact document; cursor must be 0 |

Artifact IDs include their `artifact:` prefix, for example
`hi2://artifacts/artifact:0123456789abcdef0123456789abcdef/report` (illustrative,
not an actual registration). No `file://` resources, weight bytes, config filenames,
private cache files, or client-selected log paths are served. Supported evidence
names are exactly `manifest`, `report`, `quality`, and `support`; other names fail.
Every read rechecks the registration and roots. Symlink/device/hardlink evidence
files are refused rather than followed.

Every resource content payload is capped at **32768 UTF-8 bytes**, including its
JSON envelope. File pages return `encoding: "base64"`, `data`, `cursor`,
`next_cursor`, `size_bytes`, and `eof`. Base64 preserves exact UTF-8/multibyte
boundaries and arbitrary log bytes; decode each page before interpreting the
concatenated file. `limit` is a raw-byte count, not encoded length. Cursors must
be integers in `[0, 2**53 - 1]`. Support/status are compact documents, not file
pages. Missing reports are errors, not fabricated empty reports. A log not yet
created for queued/cancelling/cancelled work reports `available: false`.
An allowed cursor at or beyond the current file size returns an empty page with
`eof: true` and the requested cursor unchanged. It does not seek beyond EOF, so
the protocol's cursor range does not depend on a filesystem's maximum seek offset.

```python
import base64


async def print_log(client, log_uri):
    cursor = 0
    while True:
        result = await client.read_resource(f"{log_uri}?cursor={cursor}&limit=4096")
        page = json.loads(result.contents[0].text)
        print(base64.b64decode(page["data"]).decode("utf-8", errors="replace"), end="")
        cursor = page["next_cursor"]
        if page["eof"]:
            return cursor
```

For a running log, `eof` means "caught up at this read", not that the worker is
finished; poll job status and resume from the saved byte cursor. Event pages
contain `next_cursor`, `oldest_cursor`, `last_cursor`, and `events_truncated`.
The job registry retains at most 1000 events per job, so an old cursor may have
missed history; do not claim it is a full audit trail. The byte cap may reduce an
event page below the requested count. An oversized individual event becomes an
explicit omission marker rather than an over-limit response. Real worker progress
is also appended as JSON lines to `worker.log`, alongside stdout/stderr and
tracebacks, so event rollover does not erase early diagnostics. Worker logs remain
on disk until operator cleanup; bounded responses are not a disk-retention quota.

## Optional negotiated Tasks

Add `--tasks` only if needed. The server then advertises
`io.modelcontextprotocol/tasks`; it does not require a client to use it. On
protocol `2026-07-28`, only callers that explicitly negotiate the extension in
per-request client capabilities may receive `resultType: "task"` from
`hi2_quantize` or `hi2_evaluate`. Ordinary clients, including the script above,
still receive ordinary `CallToolResult`/`JobHandle` responses even with the flag.

Tasks wraps the **same already-submitted durable job**; `taskId == job_id`. There
is no extra job store, executor, or second worker. `tasks/get` projects queued,
running, and cancelling jobs as `working`; succeeded jobs become `completed`.
Tool/domain failures (`failed`/`interrupted` jobs) become completed task results
with `isError: true`, rather than being mislabelled JSON-RPC task failures.
Cancelled jobs become `cancelled`. Completed results contain the same compact
job-handle schema and evidence URIs as ordinary tools.

Poll at the suggested `pollIntervalMs: 1000`. `ttlMs: null` means no automatic
expiry: receipts/registry remain until the operator removes state. Reconnect
with the same roots/state and task ID. The extension is polling-only here; it
has no input requests or task notification stream. `tasks/update` ignores
unknown/already-satisfied input response keys for existing jobs. `tasks/cancel`
acknowledges cancellation **intent**, not worker termination; keep polling.
Do not interpret a cancellation acknowledgement as released resource ownership.

See the [official Tasks overview](https://modelcontextprotocol.io/extensions/tasks/overview.md)
and [official Python SDK client documentation](https://py.sdk.modelcontextprotocol.io/client/index.md)
for client extension negotiation. Legacy experimental Tasks APIs are not this
adapter's contract.

## Troubleshooting

- **Missing extra or wrong SDK:** launch with the same interpreter used for
  installation; install `hi2[mcp]` and confirm the pinned `mcp==2.3.0`. The
  `hi2-mcp` entrypoint has no dependency on an unrelated third-party FastMCP package.
- **No output when launched in a terminal:** stdio waits for an MCP client on
  stdin. Do not type shell commands into it. Help/errors go to stderr.
- **Protocol corruption:** launch the entrypoint directly; do not place an
  `echo`, model import, or banner-writing wrapper before it. The entrypoint saves
  the original stdout wire before importing the SDK/model code, permanently
  redirects real fd 1 plus Python stdout to stderr, and passes the saved wire
  explicitly to the SDK. This includes native writes and shutdown-time flushes;
  worker output is independently captured in the job log. Code that runs before
  the entrypoint is outside that boundary.
- **Root denial / unknown ID:** use absolute local paths under the matching
  operator root; IDs are kind-scoped. Model, output, and artifact IDs are not
  interchangeable. Register a completed output using
  `hi2_inspect(kind="artifact", path=...)`. Reuse the same state root on reconnect.
- **Invalid recipe:** use the versioned `schema_version/config` envelope and
  discovered schema. Do not paste CLI-only flags, credentials, remote-code
  options, shell expressions, or `workdir`/`cache_dir`/`calib_ids` into it.
- **Busy / cancelling job:** inspect status/events/logs. Shared leases prevent
  conflicting writers; cancelling does not release ownership before actual exit.
  Never bypass a lease by deleting live state.
- **No quality evidence:** metadata inspection and structural validation do not
  measure quality. Submit `hi2_evaluate` with appropriate held-out text, then
  inspect the status and quality report. Native serving support remains unmeasured.
- **Resource page rejected:** retain the ID, use a supported resource kind, and
  keep cursor/limit within the advertised bounds. A report may not exist yet or
  may have been removed; a URI is not a capability to read other files instead.


<!-- docs/moe.md -->
# HI2 for mixture-of-experts models

`hi2`'s MoE PTQ path calibrates, allocates and encodes routed experts into the
v3.2 flex format (`quant_method: rdna_hint_flex`). A per-expert projection is an
ordinary `model.layers.N.mlp.experts.E.{gate,up,down}_proj.hi2_*` tensor; the
current default instead groups experts into stacks per layer/projection group.
The tensor decode semantics are unchanged, but a native loader must support the
selected layout. Routers, norms, embeddings and heads stay dense.
This note records the implemented paths and the measured OLMoE-1B-7B-0924 PTQ
case, not general MoE QAD or runtime compatibility. Check the
[support matrix](getting-started.md#supported-inputs-and-evidence) first.

## How experts are handled

| Step | What the tool does | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Model view | transformers 5 builds OLMoE (and Qwen2-MoE, Mixtral, ...) with fused 3-D experts (`experts.gate_up_proj [E, 2I, H]`, `experts.down_proj [E, H, I]`). `hi2.moe.unfuse_experts` replaces that module by a ModuleList of per-expert `gate_proj` / `up_proj` / `down_proj` Linears, views of the same storage, with the same forward. The first expert views are compared bit for bit with the checkpoint tensors before calibration. | The module paths are the checkpoint names, so the dense machinery (hooks, resolver, writer) applies unchanged. Checkpoints with per-expert ModuleLists need no adapter. |
| Statistics | An expert Linear runs only on the tokens routed to it, so its 16x16 block moments and its Hessian are those tokens' only. | Spec 8.7: per-expert statistics. |
| Shared inputs | Linears receiving the very same tensor (q/k/v; gate/up of one expert) are detected on their first call, which for an expert can be any sequence, and share one accumulator and one Hessian (stored once, the others are symlinks). They get one smoothing vector and one set of rotation flags (`calibrate.shared_transform`, chosen on their rows together under `--rotation auto`). | The fork rejects an expert whose gate and up transforms differ, and fuses q/k/v and gate/up into one call when they match. |
| Token floor | An expert that saw fewer than `--expert-min-tokens` (default 2) x its widest input routed tokens keeps its own tokens and fills the missing ones with the layer average of its projection: `S_e + (m - n_e) * S_layer / n_layer`, for the block moments and the Hessian. The affected experts are logged per layer; `workdir/expert_tokens.json` holds every expert's token count, the topped-up list and min / median / max per layer and projection. An expert group with no tokens at all falls back to data-free weights (logged). | A Hessian from a few hundred tokens is rank deficient; the layer average is the best prior for the missing directions. |
| Allocation weight | The allocator minimises `sum_t w_t * rel_err_t`. A dense tensor has `w = 1`; the E experts of one layer and projection get `w_e = share_e / E` with `share_e = n_e / mean(n)` (floored at `--expert-share-floor`, 0.05). `--expert-load-power p` (default 1: the weight above to the bit) raises the share to p and keeps the group at 1: `w_e = share_e^p / sum_j share_j^p`; p = 0 weighs every expert of the group alike, 0 < p < 1 flattens the allocation across experts (less load-driven INT2 on the rarely routed ones). | Each token sees a router-weighted average of its experts' errors, so the whole group weighs as one dense projection and an expert counts in proportion to the tokens it serves. Without this, 3072 expert tensors would outvote 64 attention tensors. |
| Sensitivity probe | `--sensitivity probe` quantizes a unit at the reference configuration and measures the KL: a dense tensor alone, or all experts of a layer and projection at once (112 probes for OLMoE instead of 3136). The unit's `s_u = dKL / sum_e w_e rel_err_e` is median-normalised and floored, and each member gets `s_u * w_e`. `--sens-dtype float16` puts a model that does not fit in fp32 on one GPU (bf16 has no GEMM on gfx1030 and is emulated very slowly). | Keeps the probe one forward per unit. |
| Reuse | Compatible persistent statistics/tables/probe entries can serve several allocation targets. Current output/workdir reuse also requires the matching run manifest; use separate output/work directories for a changed allocation rather than relying only on `phase1.json`. | Phase 1 and the probe were 25 of the 100 minutes in the first historical OLMoE run. |
| Writer and export | The checkpoint is streamed as before; `load_dequantized`, `export-dense`, `eval` and `verify` work on the per-expert names (transformers fuses them again on load) in either layout. `eval --dtype` selects the dtype of both models. `--moe-layout stacked` writes one stack per layer and gate_up/down group instead (see "Stacked expert layout"). | |

`UnfusedExperts` keeps the forward `down(act_fn(gate) * up)` and the hidden-dtype sum of the
top-k outputs for every model above. An `act_fn` that takes both projections, `act_fn(gate, up)`
(the soft-capped SwiGLU of Inkling-2), is detected by its signature
(`hi2.moe.is_gated_activation`) and gets the forward of its transformers class instead: the
activation on both projections and the top-k outputs added in float32
([inkling-source.md](inkling-source.md)).

## Historical PTQ recipe

This reproduces the sampling and PTQ intent of the 2026-10-26 OLMoE experiment,
not a qualified MoE QAD recipe. `$OLMOE` must name a local safetensors checkpoint
with per-expert 2-D weights. `calib_mix.txt` needs at least 364,544 tokens counted
with that model's tokenizer; `eval_mix.txt` is a separate untouched corpus.
The explicit per-expert layout matches the original experiment; new quantizations
otherwise default to stacked.

```bash
python -m hi2 quantize "$OLMOE" out-r4 --workdir olmoe-r4.work --devices cuda:0 \
  --calib calib_mix.txt --calib-samples 178 --seq-len 2048 --no-qad \
  --sensitivity probe --sens-dtype float16 --max-error-ratio 4 --moe-layout per-expert
python -m hi2 quantize "$OLMOE" out-b3.0 --workdir olmoe-b3.0.work --devices cuda:0 \
  --calib calib_mix.txt --calib-samples 178 --seq-len 2048 --no-qad \
  --sensitivity probe --sens-dtype float16 --target-bpw 3.0 --moe-layout per-expert
python -m hi2 eval "$OLMOE" out-r4 --text eval_mix.txt --skip-tokens 0 \
  --device cpu --dtype float32
```

The second allocation can reuse compatible persistent statistics and probe entries,
while keeping separate output/work state. Both commands run PTQ only: no layer-wise
QAD or final-KL. Without `--no-qad`, current default QAD requires 540,672 tokens,
more than the historical corpus. See the [support matrix](getting-started.md#supported-inputs-and-evidence)
and [recovery rules](operations.md#resume-and-invalidation) before changing a recipe.

## OLMoE-1B-7B-0924 (measured 2026-10-26)

Setup: one V620 (gfx1030), calibration on the head of `calib_mix.txt` (docs, chat and
tool-calling text, 178 x 2048 = 364,544 tokens, the whole file), `--rounding gptq`
(default), smoothing 0.5 + rotation on, `--sensitivity probe --sens-dtype float16`,
token floor 2 x input width. Evaluation with `hi2 eval` in fp32 on CPU, 8 x 2048
tokens: `study_text_all.txt` from token 800,000 and `eval_mix.txt` from token 0.
Checkpoints: `olmoe-r4` and `olmoe-b3.0`.

| Run | bpw (HI2 tensors) | bpw (whole file) | INT2 / INT4 rows | experts bpw | attention bpw | allocation error ratio |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `--max-error-ratio 4` | 3.539 | 3.97 | 40.0% / 60.0% | 3.49 | 4.67 | 4.00 |
| `--target-bpw 3.0` | 3.000 | 3.45 | 67.4% / 32.6% | 2.93 | 4.57 | 7.43 |

"Whole file" is the checkpoint size (3.44 GB and 2.98 GB, against 13.84 GB in bf16)
over all 6.92 B parameters, with embedding, head, router and norms kept in bf16.

| Run | study ppl (orig 6.634) | ratio | KLD (nats/token) | top-1 | mix ppl (orig 3.924) | ratio | KLD | top-1 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ratio 4 (3.54 bpw) | 6.707 | 1.011 | 0.0169 | 94.4% | 4.108 | 1.047 | 0.0480 | 90.2% |
| 3.0 bpw | 6.773 | 1.021 | 0.0313 | 91.8% | 4.287 | 1.093 | 0.0910 | 87.0% |

The chat and tool-calling text (`eval_mix`) loses about three times as much as the
prose text at both sizes.

Per projection (bpw, ratio 4 / 3.0 bpw): q 4.32 / 4.15, k 4.30 / 4.14, v 5.03 / 4.99,
o 5.03 / 4.97; experts gate 3.29 / 2.79, up 3.52 / 2.94, down 3.66 / 3.07. The probe
(sum over a unit, mean over layers, median 1) rates the expert groups (gate 1.16, up
1.37, down 1.40) and v/o (1.28, 1.15) well above q/k (0.37, 0.43).

### Expert coverage

Routed tokens per expert (gate, up and down of an expert see the same tokens), out of
364,544 x 8 routed slots per layer (45,568 per expert if uniform):

| Layer | min | median | max | Layer | min | median | max |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 0 | 5,582 | 39,860 | 178,164 | 8 | 4,964 | 36,414 | 224,103 |
| 1 | 11,763 | 38,042 | 207,533 | 9 | 9,338 | 36,551 | 218,736 |
| 2 | 8,457 | 37,162 | 241,330 | 10 | 5,251 | 36,785 | 203,442 |
| 3 | 11,036 | 36,860 | 167,767 | 11 | 8,942 | 33,717 | 222,244 |
| 4 | 5,206 | 32,071 | 195,731 | 12 | 5,375 | 35,151 | 202,658 |
| 5 | 9,361 | 31,200 | 227,366 | 13 | 6,921 | 33,321 | 179,215 |
| 6 | 8,113 | 33,054 | 211,453 | 14 | 4,865 | 35,072 | 206,220 |
| 7 | 3,358 | 34,442 | 223,271 | 15 | 6,122 | 31,146 | 207,265 |

Every one of the 1,024 experts received tokens; 12 got fewer than 8,192 and one (layer
7, expert 51, 3,358 tokens) was below the 4,096-token floor and topped up (its three
tensors). Routing is skewed: the busiest expert of a layer sees 4 to 7 times the median.
With the 16k-token calibration of a smoke test, 63 of 64 layer-0 experts were below the
floor, so the full calibration file is needed.

### Cost

| Stage | Time | Peak memory |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Phase 1 (calibration, Hessians) | 15.5 min | host RSS about 58 GiB (fp32 model in RAM) |
| Sensitivity probe (112 units, fp16 model on the GPU) | about 10 min | GPU 13.8 GiB |
| Phase 3, ratio 4 | 72 min | host 37 GiB (run with the probe) |
| Phase 3, 3.0 bpw (phase 1 and probe reused) | 75 min | host 7.5 GiB |

The historical run's per-expert Hessians (gate/up shared, fp32) took about 21 GB
on disk and were deleted after its second allocation; this is not a cleanup
recommendation. The current persistent cache retains reusable statistics; its
measured OLMoE entry is 11.8 GB ([speed.md](speed.md#disk)). The remaining historical
workdir was 0.8 GB. CPU fp32 evaluation took about 15 minutes per text.

## Stacked expert layout (`--moe-layout stacked`)

The historical per-expert layout writes every expert projection as its own HI2
tensor: nine arrays per tensor, 28,307 tensors and a 3.5 MB safetensors header for
OLMoE-1B-7B. In that measurement the fork's loader concatenated 64 experts per
stage and rebased their offsets, taking about 95 s on a quiet host and 350-560 s
under load (dense fp16: 22 s). The **current default is stacked**, which writes
the experts of one layer and projection group as one set of arrays already in
the layout the fork builds in memory. It changes storage only. Every expert's
arrays and dequantized weights matched in the measured round trips below.
The spec container of a single tensor (spec 2.4) is unchanged.

* `hi2 quantize` writes the stacked layout by default (`--moe-layout per-expert` for the
  old one): the fork loader reads stacks, and model load drops from 72-94 s to 9-16 s on
  OLMoE with identical tensors and outputs. The stacked run writes per expert
  into `<workdir>/per-expert/<dst name>`, then restacks into `dst` and removes the
  staging copy.
* `hi2 restack CKPT OUT --layout stacked|per-expert` converts an existing checkpoint
  either way without re-encoding (byte-level data movement). It took 30 s and 4.9 GB RSS
  for `olmoe-r4`.
* `hi2 verify`, `hi2 export-dense`, `hi2 eval` and `checkpoint.load_dequantized` read
  both layouts, and so does `hi2.stack.Hi2Checkpoint`, which gives per-expert access
  by logical name.

### Names

A stack is `<pre>.<group>.hi2_<field>`. `<pre>` is the experts prefix of one layer
(`model.layers.L.mlp.experts`) and `<group>` is one of:

| group | parts, in row order | fork stage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `gate_up` | `gate_proj`, `up_proj` | `w13` |
| `down` | `down_proj` | `w2` |
| `w13` (Mixtral names) | `w1`, `w3` | `w13` |
| `w2` (Mixtral names) | `w2` | `w2` |

A stack is recognised by its `hi2_part_off` array. A single HI2 tensor never has one.
`config.json` records the layout in `quantization_config`:
`"moe_layout": "stacked"` and `"moe_stacks": {"down": ["down_proj"], "gate_up":
["gate_proj", "up_proj"]}`, the groups used and their part order. `moe_layout` is
`per-expert` (and `moe_stacks` absent) after a restack back. Checkpoints written before
this change have no `moe_layout` key, which means per-expert.

### Arrays

`E` experts, `P` parts per expert (2 for `gate_up`, 1 for `down`), `K` input width
(hidden size for `gate_up`, intermediate size for `down`). Slice `j = e * P + p` is
part `p` of expert `e`, and `R` is the total row count. For OLMoE, `gate_up` has
E = 64, K = 2048 and R = 64 x 2 x 1024 = 131,072, and `down` has K = 1024 and
R = 64 x 2048 = 131,072.

| field | dtype | shape | content |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `hi2_codes` | int32 | `[sum words]` | the slices' `codes`, concatenated in slice order |
| `hi2_code_off` | int32 | `[R + 1]` | word offset of each row in `hi2_codes`, rebased to the concatenation |
| `hi2_blk_params` | uint8 | `[2 * sum blocks]` | the slices' `(lo_u8, step_u8)` pairs, concatenated |
| `hi2_param_off` | int32 | `[R + 1]` | block offset of each row (byte offset / 2), rebased |
| `hi2_row_consts` | fp16 | `[R, 3]` | `lo_min, lo_scale, step_scale` per row, concatenated |
| `hi2_row_nbits` | uint8 | `[R]` | code width per row |
| `hi2_row_block` | uint8 | `[R]` | block size per row |
| `hi2_had` | uint8 | `[E, K/16]` | rotation flags of expert `e` (shared by its parts) |
| `hi2_col_scale` | fp16 | `[E, K]` | smoothing vector of expert `e`; present iff the experts are smoothed |
| `hi2_part_off` | int32 | `[E * P + 1]` | first row of slice `j`; slice `j` is rows `[part_off[j], part_off[j+1])` |

### Invariants a loader can rely on

1. The seven row arrays form one valid flat container of `R` rows (spec 2.4).
   `code_off[0] = param_off[0] = 0`. `code_off[r+1] - code_off[r] = K * nbits_r / 32`
   and `param_off[r+1] - param_off[r] = K / block_r`. `code_off[R]` is the length of
   `hi2_codes` and `2 * param_off[R]` is the length of `hi2_blk_params`. The fork's
   `validate()` passes on them if `K` is taken as `had.shape[1] * 16`, not
   `had.numel() * 16`. All offsets fit int32; the writer keeps a group per expert
   rather than overflow.
2. `E = had.shape[0] = col_scale.shape[0]` and `part_off.numel() = E * P + 1`. Every
   expert and part of a stack has the same `K`.
3. `part_off[0] = 0` and `part_off[E * P] = R`. The parts of a group have equal row
   counts in every model the tool handles: OLMoE gate/up 1024 rows, down 2048 rows. The
   table states the counts, so a loader should read it, not assume them.
4. Row order is expert-major, part-minor. For `gate_up`, expert `e`'s rows are
   `[part_off[2e], part_off[2e+2])`, gate first. This is the fork's in-memory `w13`
   (rows `[2Ne, 2N(e+1))`, gate first), and `down` is its `w2`
   (rows `[He, H(e+1))`).
5. Gate and up of an expert share one transform (decision 6), so `had` and
   `col_scale` have one row per expert, as the fork's `[E, K/16]` / `[E, K]` tables do.
   `col_scale` is all-or-none per stack. If it is absent, there is no smoothing (the
   fork's empty `col_scale`).
6. Slicing a stack gives the per-expert tensors back byte for byte. Rows
   `[part_off[j], part_off[j+1])` of the row arrays, `codes[code_off[r0]:code_off[r1]]`,
   `blk_params[2*param_off[r0]:2*param_off[r1]]`, the offsets minus their first entry,
   `had[e]` and `col_scale[e]` are exactly the per-expert arrays. This is the fork's
   `select_rows`.
7. The two stacks of a layer are in one shard: the shard that held most of that
   layer's expert tensors. Dense tensors keep their shard.
8. A group is stacked only if experts `0..E-1` all have every part, gate and up of
   each expert share `had` and `col_scale`, and smoothing is on for all experts of the
   stack or for none. Otherwise that group stays per expert in the same checkpoint and
   the tool logs it. A loader must accept both forms per layer. No tool-made checkpoint
   has such a group so far.

`hi2_report.json` keeps one entry per expert projection under `tensors`, under the
per-expert names (bpw, error, INT4 share). `summary.moe_layout` and
`summary.moe_stacks` (the stack count) record the layout. `model.safetensors.index.json`
lists the stack arrays, and its `metadata.total_size` is recomputed.

### What the fork loader has to do

* Accept `<pre>.gate_up.hi2_*` and `<pre>.down.hi2_*` (and `w13` / `w2`) as whole-stage
  tensors for the layer's `RoutedExperts`. The `hi2_part_off` array marks a stack.
* At TP = 1 with all experts local, use the arrays as `_rdna_hint_flex_w13` / `_w2`
  directly: `col_scale` is `[E, K]` or absent (pass the empty tensor), and `had` is
  `[E, K/16]`. `stack_experts` and the offset rebase are no longer needed.
* At TP > 1, take each expert's gate and up slices from `part_off`, then select rank
  rows `[rank*n, (rank+1)*n)` of each and the rank columns of `down` with the
  existing `select_rows` / `select_cols`. Concatenate in the same order. With expert
  parallelism, take the local experts' rows `[part_off[e0*P], part_off[e1*P])` and
  `had[e0:e1]`, `col_scale[e0:e1]`.
* Keep the per-expert path for checkpoints without stacks and for groups the writer
  left per expert.

### OLMoE-1B-7B, restacked (measured 2026-10-26)

`hi2 restack` turned the two checkpoints into
`olmoe-r4-stacked` and `olmoe-b3.0-stacked`, both without re-encoding.

| Checkpoint | Layout | Tensors | Safetensors headers | Index file | Shard files |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `olmoe-r4` | per-expert | 28,307 | 3,486,304 B | 2,699,532 B | 3,430,904,600 B |
| `olmoe-r4-stacked` | stacked | **979** | **114,408 B** | **86,500 B** | 3,423,195,424 B |
| `olmoe-b3.0` | per-expert | 28,307 | 3,472,296 B | 2,699,532 B | 2,978,556,352 B |
| `olmoe-b3.0-stacked` | stacked | **979** | **114,176 B** | **86,500 B** | 2,970,860,952 B |

979 tensors = 32 stacks x 10 arrays + 64 attention projections x 9 arrays + 83 dense
tensors (embedding, head, norms, routers). The stacked shards are 7.7 MB smaller. Of
that, 3.4 MB is header. The rest is the transform that gate and up stored twice per
expert and now store once (`col_scale` 4 KB and `had` 128 B per expert). Each layer's
two stacks sit in one of the three shards (365 / 366 / 248 tensors). Each restack took
about 30 s and under 5 GB RSS.

`experiments/compare_layouts.py` checks every one of the 3,136 logical HI2 tensors
(3,072 expert projections and 64 attention projections). For each, all nine arrays are
equal in dtype, shape and bytes, and the float64 dequantization in the original
basis is `torch.equal`. The 83 dense tensors are identical too. The stacked
checkpoint restacked back to per-expert gives the original arrays byte for byte.

`hi2 eval` (fp32 on CPU, 8 x 2048 tokens) of the originals and the stacked copies,
run side by side with the same settings:

| Checkpoint | study ppl | study KLD | study top-1 | mix ppl | mix KLD | mix top-1 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `olmoe-r4` | 6.706628799 | 0.016882055 | 0.944064485 | 4.107589722 | 0.047960081 | 0.902051783 |
| `olmoe-r4-stacked` | 6.706628799 | 0.016882055 | 0.944064485 | 4.107589722 | 0.047960081 | 0.902051783 |
| `olmoe-b3.0` | 6.772842407 | 0.031327991 | 0.917867611 | 4.287377834 | 0.090997412 | 0.870114802 |
| `olmoe-b3.0-stacked` | 6.772842407 | 0.031327991 | 0.917867611 | 4.287377834 | 0.090997412 | 0.870114802 |

All printed digits are identical, and the values match the references (6.707 / 4.108 and
6.773 / 4.287).

### Layout tests

`tests/test_stack.py` checks the stack invariants (dtypes, shapes, rebased offsets,
`part_off`, one flat container whose rows dequantize as the experts') and the
byte-exact split. It checks the rejection of unstackable groups (rotation or smoothing
that differs, mixed smoothing, different K), and a two-shard checkpoint (one layer
split across shards) converted per-expert -> stacked -> per-expert: equal arrays,
equal `load_dequantized`, one shard per layer, config/report/index. It also checks
that an unstackable group stays per expert. `tests/test_moe.py` quantizes the tiny
OLMoE with `--moe-layout stacked` and per expert from one phase 1. It checks equal
arrays for every tensor, equal `load_dequantized`, `verify` on the stacked checkpoint,
SHA-256-identical `export-dense` files, and `restack` both ways.

## Tests

`tests/test_moe.py` (CPU): a toy MoE with controlled routing checks that each expert's
statistics and Hessian equal those of its routed tokens, that a nearly unused expert is
topped up with exactly `own + (m - n) x layer average` and reported, that q/k/v and
gate/up share smoothing and flags (rotation `on` and `auto`), the allocation weights,
and the unfused forward against the fused one; a tiny OLMoE runs end to end and checks
the tensor names, the dense router, shared transforms in the written file, and the
export round trip through transformers.

## Implementation status and remaining limits

* Fused/batched GPTQ encoding is implemented, not future work: the OLMoE
  measurement fell from roughly 4,450 s to 192 s for 3,136 tensors on one V620,
  with byte-identical output. See [the implemented changes](speed.md#what-changed)
  and the OLMoE table under [speed measurements](speed.md#measurements).
* Native OLMoE serving was measured in the external fork, including both expert
  layouts; see [the recorded serving results](results.md#olmoe-1b-7b-unquantized-ppl-6634-docs-3924-mix-whole-file-1384-gb)
  and the layout measurements above. That is historical evidence, not a
  version-pinned installation recipe for arbitrary vLLM builds.
* Checkpoints storing fused 3-D expert weights on disk are not split by the
  writer. In-memory Transformers fusion adapted from per-expert 2-D source
  tensors is a different, implemented case. Check the actual files rather than
  inferring layout from a model-family name.
* Qwen2-MoE/Mixtral naming and adaptation paths exist, but are not equivalent to
  a measured end-to-end recipe for every revision. Shared experts are ordinary
  dense Linears in the implementation and remain unqualified by the OLMoE study.
* MoE QAD and MoE final-KL have no published end-to-end quality measurements here.
  The dense-model QAD results do not establish those combinations.


<!-- docs/operations.md -->
# Resources, recovery and distribution

Use [getting started](getting-started.md) for an ordered run and
[usage.md](usage.md) for flags. This page separates implemented lifecycle behavior
from estimates and historical measurements. No new GPU or native-runtime
validation was performed for these documentation changes.

## Read the plan without overreading it

Run the **same intended command** with `--dry-run` first. The resolved pipeline
checks supported numeric/configuration values, incompatible options, requested
CPU/CUDA device availability, input/output path aliases, model metadata and
indexed safetensors presence, and calibration token requirements. It reports
resolved settings, output destinations and requested phases. It does not execute
model forward/backward passes or native loading.

The resource section leaves total runtime and peak GPU memory (VRAM) unknown.
Its QAD fp32 hidden-state estimate is roughly
`4 buffers * (training + validation sequences) * seq_len * hidden_size * 4 bytes`.
This is not the full resident process memory (RSS). Source weights, live layer
copies, Hessians, optimizer state and logits need additional memory.
A successful dry-run does not reserve RAM, VRAM or disk space. Readable metadata
also does not prove that Transformers can execute the architecture.
Source: [`service.resolve_config`](../hi2/service.py) and
[`pipeline.plan`](../hi2/pipeline.py).

Dry-run performs read-only ownership/identity checks for existing work/output
paths, so an incompatible resume can fail during planning. It is not a durable
ownership claim: execution must acquire the locks and repeat those checks before
it can resume or write. Another process can change availability after planning.
There is no promise that the number of requested phases equals the number
actually completed: final-KL can be skipped by its fit check.

## Phase resource map

| Active phase / symptom | Host RAM and device demand | Disk and time considerations |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Input/tokenization, plan | Tokenizer and token IDs; shard/header inspection, not a model forward | Source/tokenizer files must be local and complete. A plan is not a capacity reservation. |
| Calibration/statistics | Source model in host memory; active decoder layers/device replicas and hidden-state buffers; per-input Hessians scale as K squared | Persistent statistics, Hessians and tables can be much larger than the output. Calibration duration is separate from QAD. |
| Sensitivity probe | Different placement from layer training: whole-model or layer-sharded probe, with retained reference probabilities/activations; `sens-dtype auto` may select fp16 for fit | Repeated model forwards; cached probe is cheap to reuse. Reducing QAD buffer dtype does not solve a probe-placement OOM. |
| Allocation | Candidate tables and picks, primarily host data | Usually small compared with probe/training; allocation bpw is not peak RAM or actual file size. |
| GPTQ encode | Active weight/candidate batches, Hessian transforms and factorization; source-shard staging | Reads cached statistics, writes output shards or per-expert staging. First-use fused compilation is extra. |
| Layer-wise QAD | Source model/teacher-student hidden states in RAM; live layer, fp32 latents, ladder parameters, optimizer state and optional replicas on devices | Completed `layerNNN.pt` artifacts; saved teacher `head_inputs.pt` when produced. The printed QAD time estimate covers layer training, not final-KL or total execution. |
| Final logit-KL | Whole-network student split layer-wise; teacher placement avoided when saved head inputs are available; optimizer/activation/logit headroom still required | Separate phase with `final_kl.pt` and report. At 27B this phase took longer than layer-wise QAD. CPU is not protected by a GPU-fit estimate. |
| Publish/restack | Shard/container processing and staging, not just one model layer | Stacked output can coexist with its per-expert staging copy until publication finishes. Plan space for both. |
| Structural validation / sampled `verify` | Scans containers or reconstructs selected tensors; no full model inference | Structural validation and numerical reconstruction coverage are distinct. See [evaluation](evaluation.md#verification-coverage). |
| `eval` | Reference and dense reconstructed models loaded in turn, whole-model or layer-sharded; reference vocabulary log-probabilities retained on CPU | Temporary dense fp16 checkpoint plus its load-time state; this can dominate disk/RAM even if the HI2 file is small. |
| `export-dense` | Dequantized state dictionary and fp16 conversion can coexist in RAM | Full dense checkpoint on disk, not compressed inference. |

For a rough disk envelope, add **source + cache + work artifacts + staging + all
requested outputs + temporary/persistent dense copies + portable asset copies**.
Budget the peak overlap, not only the final output. QAD ratio sweeps repeat the
QAD/final-KL work and keep per-output artifacts; compatible statistics/probes can
be shared. Do not assume a single layerwise memory model applies to every phase.

### Measured examples, not universal estimates

* The [speed study](speed.md#disk) reports persistent statistics of about 0.9 GB
  for Qwen3.5-0.8B, 7.0 GB for 4B and 11.8 GB for OLMoE. These are that study's
  entries, not all working/output disk. Original per-workdir Hessians were larger.
* Historical one-V620 QAD measurements report about 15 GiB process RAM/1.4 GiB
  GPU peak at 0.8B and 46 GiB/6.0 GiB at 4B during layer-wise training. They do
  not bound probe, final-KL or evaluation memory ([qad.md](qad.md)).
* The completed 27B `default` run on four V620s took **247 minutes layer-wise +
  470.8 minutes final-KL**. Saved teacher head inputs were about 10.3 GiB. These
  are distinct from the compressed 15 GiB checkpoint ([surfaces.md](surfaces.md#the-default-27b-run-finished-2026-10-29)).
* `qad.estimate_seconds` scales measured layer-training work by tensor shapes and
  tokens; its CPU multiplier is a heuristic, not a CPU benchmark. Do not report
  it as a measured total-job ETA.

Final-KL means the final **logit-KL training phase**, not independent quality
evaluation. The `default` and `thorough` presets request it. The `fast` preset
and explicit `--qad-final-kl 0` disable it.

On GPU, the fit check can skip this phase if the estimated per-device memory
need remains too large after sharding. The estimate uses nominal capacity and
headroom. It does not guarantee free VRAM or prevent every out-of-memory (OOM)
failure. Retain the skip message and phase status; do not label that output
“final-KL trained.” `final_tokens` reduces positions in the loss, not the memory
needed to place model weights. Even a completed phase needs independent
[final quality evaluation](evaluation.md#allocation-and-kld-targets).

## First-use and slow starts

Fused HIP GPTQ/refinement compilation, model-specific Triton compilation and
GEMM autotuning are different costs. The V620 studies reported roughly 40-50 s
for a fused extension build and around ten minutes for the first linear-attention
backward autotune, sometimes longer for a new dtype. Preserve compatible build
caches; expect a different compiler, device or dtype to require new compilation.
Read [installation](installation.md#first-use-compilation-and-smoke) for eligible
architectures and Torch fallback controls. A long first layer alone is not proof
of a deadlock; inspect the current phase/compiler activity and available memory
before stopping only the process you own.

## Artifact ownership

The **output** is the checkpoint you evaluate or distribute. The **workdir**
holds one run's recovery state. The **persistent cache** holds compatible phase
products for reuse across runs. None of these directories replaces the others.

Keep persistent cache, workdir and output trees separate from one another and from
source/calibration inputs. Preflight rejects equality, containment and aliases
before writes: an output nested under a cache is unsafe because pruning owns cache
products. `--no-cache` instead keeps phase products inside the owned workdir.

| Artifact | Role and retention |
| --- | --- |
| Local source checkpoint, tokenizer and calibration inputs | Immutable inputs for a repeatable run; retain provenance independently of cache/work state. |
| Persistent cache (`--cache-dir`, `$HI2_CACHE_DIR` or `~/.cache/hi2`) | Reusable statistics/Hessians/tables/probe products under their keyed identities; see [speed.md](speed.md#the-cache). |
| `<workdir>/run.json` | Versioned run identity, resolved configuration, outputs, lifecycle/phase record. Do not edit it to bypass ownership checks. |
| `<workdir>/.run.lock` and output-side lock files | Exclusive writer coordination. A lock filename's existence alone does not mean a live owner; do not remove lock files to evade a lock. |
| `cache_ref.json`, phase tables and sensitivity records | References or phase products used by this run. `--no-cache` uses workdir-local products instead. |
| `qad/layerNNN.pt`, `qad/head_inputs.pt`, `qad/final_kl.pt` | Completed layer states, saved teacher head inputs and completed final-KL state; multi-ratio runs use `qad-r<ratio>` namespaces. |
| Per-expert staging and shard/report recovery artifacts | Intermediate publication state; preserve during an unchanged interrupted run. Do not mix it into another run's output. |
| Output `.hi2-owner.json`, `hi2_report.json`, `hi2_run.json` | Ownership, tensor/quality-accounting report and successful run record. A directory's mere presence is not completion. |
| Temporary evaluation/tuning dense products | Scratch space owned by that invocation, not a user checkpoint or a substitute for persistent diagnostics. Never delete similarly named user data as cleanup. |

These are cooperative POSIX locks, not filesystem permissions or an OS sandbox.
Do not unlink their files: a live owner may still hold the old inode while a new
process locks a different one. A recorded PID, heartbeat or `running` state alone
is not proof of liveness. Keep state on a filesystem with working local advisory
locks; multi-host ownership is not qualified.

## Resume and invalidation

Execution takes exclusive ownership of the workdir and output paths.
The `run_id` identifies resolved settings, inputs and implementation environment.
A compatible resume keeps that ID and can reuse committed phase products.
Each execution gets a fresh `attempt_id`. A durable submission has a separate
`job_id`; see [the identity fields](usage.md#cache-and-work-directory).

A different run identity is rejected rather than mixed with prior output.
A nonempty legacy workdir without a valid run manifest is not automatically
adopted. The pipeline structurally validates the output before publishing its
success record. Each artifact replacement is atomic, but publication across
all output markers and `run.json` is not one atomic transaction.
Ordinary publication exceptions restore prior success markers. A hard kill
can leave partial publication or a stale `running` record. A marker alone
neither repairs corruption nor proves that the current attempt finished.

Recovery granularity is phase-specific:

* Calibration/statistics/probe reuse complete compatible cache products.
* QAD reuses completed layers; the interrupted layer starts again. There is no
  optimizer-step resume within that layer.
* Completed final-KL has its own fingerprint and artifact. An interrupted final
  phase restarts the phase, not its last optimizer step.
* Output shard reuse must carry compatible metadata/report coverage. Do not
  create completion markers manually or copy them between destinations.

Keep the source/calibration files, resolved settings, cache and work/output
locations unchanged when rerunning after interruption. For changed recipes,
model weights, tokenizer or calibration, use **new output and work directories**;
compatible persistent cache products may still be reused according to their
keys. Never treat a filename, size, or leftover `.pt` as proof that it belongs to
the new run. Historical caches predating content-based identity need particular
care: their model fingerprint used config bytes and weight/index names and sizes,
not complete weight contents. Do not manually rekey old entries; use fresh state
when changing such inputs.

After stopping your foreground invocation, inspect `hi2 status WORKDIR` and
retain its logs. That command reads recorded state; it does not prove the process
has exited. Once the actual owner has exited, rerun the unchanged command to let
the locked lifecycle validate/reuse compatible state, then run `hi2 validate OUT`
before acceptance. If ownership is still busy, do not delete locks or launch a
second writer. Changes to the recorded implementation/environment can also make
resume incompatible; preserve the old run and choose fresh paths rather than
editing its identity.

For [durable MCP jobs](mcp.md), a cancellation acknowledgement means **requested**,
not **stopped**. Keep polling until the job reaches a terminal state. The worker
must exit before reconciliation releases its resource leases.
A validated commit from the job's bound `attempt_id` can win a late cancellation.
The recorded process `returncode` remains the actual exit code, not a fabricated
zero. An older successful attempt with the same `run_id` cannot complete this job.
Forced termination can prevent a worker receipt from being written. Its absence
alone does not invalidate a recoverable, validated commit from the current attempt.
Read the persisted job result and the run/artifact evidence together.
Interrupted work is not automatically replayed.

The model fingerprint uses `sha256-content-v1`. It hashes full config/index/shard
bytes and relative filenames, including nested index-referenced shards.
An unchanged copy with the same relative layout matches; same-size weight edits
do not, even with preserved timestamps. Current statistics use
`STATS_VERSION=3`, which also includes active auxiliary model inputs such as
`HI2_NGRAM_MMAP` by canonical path and full SHA-256. Older statistics keys miss,
as do the table/probe keys derived from them.
Hashing costs a full checkpoint read. Keep source and auxiliary files unchanged
throughout a run.

Preview cache pruning with `hi2 cache list`, `hi2 cache size` and
`hi2 cache prune --dry-run`. Remove only the selected obsolete products, never
unrelated checkpoints, all of a user's cache, or repository history. Source:
[`runtime.py`](../hi2/runtime.py), [`service.py`](../hi2/service.py),
[`pipeline.py`](../hi2/pipeline.py), [`cache.py`](../hi2/cache.py).

## Troubleshooting by phase

| Symptom | Focused remedy |
| --- | --- |
| Insufficient calibration/evaluation tokens | Compare available with required **usable** tokens and the model tokenizer. Add independently assigned documents or intentionally change sample/window settings; do not solve it by consuming final-test data. |
| Metadata/shard/decoder discovery failure | Confirm local safetensors layout, complete index and model/Transformers compatibility; consult the support matrix. More VRAM cannot fix an unsupported layout. |
| OOM during probing | Inspect probe dtype and model placement, not only QAD buffer settings. Re-plan with explicit devices and use a fresh run for changed settings. |
| OOM during layer QAD | Inspect source/hidden-state host RAM, layer/optimizer GPU demand and replica count; reducing buffers can change training numerics. |
| Final-KL skipped or OOM | Record the skip/failure. Its full-network placement differs from layer QAD. Disabling it is a deliberate recipe change, not equivalent completion. |
| OOM/disk-full during `eval` or dense export | Budget dense weights, reference vocabulary logits and temporary disk; small compressed file size is not the memory requirement. |
| Fused kernel build failure | Check ROCm Torch/compiler/architecture alignment, or explicitly use the Torch reference path. Do not force gfx1030 binaries on unrelated devices. |
| Incompatible/unowned workdir or output | Preserve it and use fresh named destinations. Do not erase the manifest, copy markers, or bypass the ownership check. |
| Interrupted run or corrupt artifact | Preserve logs/run identity and retry unchanged inputs for normal phase resume. For corrupted committed artifacts, isolate the affected run and use fresh work state rather than trusting or restamping it. |
| Broken tokenizer after moving output | Inspect source-asset symlinks; use a portable copy rather than assuming the HI2 directory is self-contained. |
| `Unknown quantization method: rdna_hint_flex` | The installed inference runtime lacks the needed registration/loader. Dense evaluation cannot fix or certify it; see the [native handoff boundary](getting-started.md#export-and-native-serving). |

## Distribute a checkpoint

Ordinary quantization and dense export can link tokenizer/auxiliary files to the
source directory; restacking can preserve those links. Removing or moving the
source can therefore break the output. Checkpoint tensors retaining a source
dtype is unrelated to this filesystem dependency.

For a self-contained HI2 copy, select a new destination and explicitly trust
the source root for linked assets:

```bash
hi2 export-portable OUT PORTABLE --assets-from MODEL
hi2 validate PORTABLE
```

This copies quantized shards unchanged rather than re-encoding or dequantizing
them, materializes the explicitly allowed local assets/dependencies, and records
hashes in `hi2_portable_manifest.json`.
External linked assets require an explicitly trusted source root; missing,
escaping or remote dependencies must not be silently accepted. The destination
must not exist, even as an empty directory. Export locks source and destination,
validates the source, copies into the destination, writes its manifest, then
validates the result. It is not an atomic directory publication: caught failures
remove the invocation's new directory, but a hard kill can leave a partial copy.
Never distribute a destination still being written. Retain the original until
export has returned successfully and `hi2 validate PORTABLE` passes; after an
interruption preserve diagnostics and use a different new destination.
Portability is not native-loader compatibility.

The copy preserves the artifact's `hi2_run.json` and existing `hi2_quality.json`
when present; `--assets-from` cannot supply or replace that evidence. Recorded
calibration/tuning corpora remain external inputs, not bundled datasets. Later
quality evaluation requires recorded calibration files plus any explicitly
excluded tuning files, and rejects missing inputs or exact-document overlap.
It rejects a different reference checkpoint when the run records its source
fingerprint; an artifact without that provenance does not acquire it merely by
being exported. This overlap check is not semantic deduplication.

Quality evaluation locks the artifact and updates its quality report and portable
checksum manifest together under that lock. Both accepted and rejected quality
gates leave a structurally valid bundle without changing weight bytes. An ordinary
publication exception restores the prior report/manifest. The two filesystem
replacements are not jointly crash-atomic: a hard kill between them can leave a
bundle that fails validation. Do not distribute it or bypass checksum validation;
export a fresh copy from the retained original.

The manifest covers bundle files; it is not a signature, a Python dependency
lock or a code-safety audit. Local custom-code assets may be copied with their
dependencies. Review code before enabling a runtime's trust/custom-code option;
portable packaging does not sandbox it or install external Python libraries.

`export-dense` is the other route: a dense fp16 Hugging Face checkpoint for a
compatible engine, **not GGUF** and not native low-bit HI2 inference. A llama.cpp
handoff requires a separately compatible HF-to-GGUF conversion and its own model
support checks. No version-pinned native HI2 runtime or validated inference
request is supplied by this page.


<!-- docs/qad.md -->
# HI2 quantization-aware distillation (QAD)

Tool-side extension of the HI2 v3.2 format (`format-spec.md`, section 13).
QAD changes which codes and block parameters are stored, never the format: a QAD
checkpoint is a normal `rdna_hint_flex` v3.2 checkpoint that `hi2 eval`,
`hi2 export-dense` and the fork's loader read unchanged.

## Quick start

QAD is **on by default** whenever calibration text is given:

```bash
cd .
# HF model -> QAD checkpoint (quality allocation, sensitivity probe, PTQ, then QAD)
python -m hi2 quantize <hf_model_dir> <out_dir> --calib calib.txt --max-error-ratio 6
# PTQ only
python -m hi2 quantize <hf_model_dir> <out_dir> --calib calib.txt --no-qad
# inspect inputs/settings and resource estimates; this is not a model execution
python -m hi2 quantize <hf_model_dir> <out_dir> --calib calib.txt --dry-run
# score it against the unquantized model on held-out text
python -m hi2 eval <hf_model_dir> <out_dir> --text heldout.txt --device cuda:0
# dense fp16 Hugging Face copy (llama.cpp requires a separate compatible GGUF conversion)
python -m hi2 export-dense <hf_model_dir> <out_dir> <dense_dir>
```

At the start the run logs that QAD is on and a rough estimate of layer-wise training
time on the chosen device. That estimate is not total runtime: final-KL, probing,
encoding and first-use compilation have separate costs ([operations.md](operations.md)).
A run without `--calib` (data-free) cannot distill: it logs a warning and writes a
PTQ checkpoint. `--no-qad` also selects PTQ only. `--max-error-ratio 6` is the
